People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. They said it's non-compliant because it's slightly off-white instead of eggshell. Who even notices that? My neighbor, Susan, she has this bright blue door and nobody says a word to her. Yeah, I guess so. Most of the day, every day, really. It's just this... heaviness. Like I can't even get out of bed sometimes. My bed is actually really uncomfortable, I've been meaning to get a new mattress for like, two years now. I read somewhere that you're supposed to flip them every six months, but mine is one of those pillow-top ones so you can't really flip it, you just rotate it. But it's so heavy, I can't do it by myself. 

My ex-boyfriend used to help me rotate it. He was really into fitness, always at the gym. He drank those protein shakes that smelled like fake chocolate and chalk. We used to go to this smoothie place after his workouts, and I'd always get the mango one. Mangoes are so hard to cut, though, aren't they? There's that giant pit in the middle and you end up wasting half the fruit trying to slice around it. I watched a tutorial on YouTube once about how to cut them with a glass, but I just ended up making a huge sticky mess on my kitchen counter. 

But yeah... down. Just really, really down all the time.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I don't know if it's because I feel responsible, but I do check things. A lot, actually. Like... there are times I realize I’ve just been pacing the exact same path, back and forth, from the kitchen to the front door. And I'll be rubbing my thumb against my fingers, just over and over, and I keep checking the peephole. Even though no one knocked and nothing has actually happened. 

The peephole is kind of blurry anyway, to be honest. It's one of those old brass ones that gets all tarnished and scratched up. I tried cleaning it with brass polish once, but the smell gave me this massive headache. It smelled like... I don't know, ammonia and old pennies? I hate the smell of pennies. When I was a kid, my grandpa used to keep this huge glass jar of pennies in his den, and he'd let me roll them in those little paper tubes to take to the bank. The bank tellers always gave me a lollipop for bringing them in. Usually cherry, which is the best flavor, obviously. Grape is the worst. It always tastes like cough syrup. I had to take so much grape cough syrup when I had bronchitis in college. I missed two weeks of classes and had to beg my statistics professor to let me take the midterm late. He was this really stern guy with a mustache that looked completely fake, like it was glued on. 

Anyway... yeah, I just pace and check the door. Over and over. It's exhausting, honestly.

He drank those protein shakes that smelled like fake chocolate and chalk. We used to go to this smoothie place after his workouts, and I'd always get the mango one. Mangoes are so hard to cut, though, aren't they? There's that giant pit in the middle and you end up wasting half the fruit trying to slice around it. I watched a tutorial on YouTube once about how to cut them with a glass, but I just ended up making a huge sticky mess on my kitchen counter. \n\nBut yeah... down. Just really, really down all the time.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. If you miss one little comma in a formula, the whole workbook just breaks. I had this one file completely corrupt on me right before a big meeting last year. I don't even know where you buy ties like that. Probably online. \n\nI buy almost everything online now. Oh, about feeling depressed before. Um, no. Never for weeks at a time like this. Even though no one knocked and nothing has actually happened. \n\nThe peephole is kind of blurry anyway, to be honest. It's one of those old brass ones that gets all tarnished and scratched up. It's exhausting, honestly.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you keep checking the door, is it because an unwanted thought keeps telling you something bad will happen if you don’t?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 27 / business_operations_specialist / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, it's not really a thought like that. It's not like a voice or a specific worry telling me, "Check the door or else." It's more just... this feeling. Like a physical urge to just keep doing it, even though my brain isn't really saying anything. 

My brain usually doesn't shut up, though. It's always going. Mostly about work, honestly. We just switched to this new software platform for expense reporting, and it is a complete nightmare. The interface is so clunky, and it keeps logging me out every fifteen minutes if I don't move the mouse. I bought one of those mouse jigglers online to keep it active, but it makes this weird whirring noise that drives me crazy. It sounds exactly like the old refrigerator in my first apartment. That thing used to vibrate so loudly I had to put folded up cardboard under the front legs to level it out. Cardboard boxes are so annoying to break down for recycling. You always get those tiny paper cuts right in the webbing of your fingers. 

Paper cuts are the absolute worst. I read once that they hurt so much because the edges of paper are actually like microscopic saws, so it tears the skin instead of slicing it cleanly. And there are more nerve endings in your fingertips than almost anywhere else in your body. I learned that in a biology class in college. The professor was this older woman who always wore these massive, chunky turquoise necklaces. She told us she bought them all in New Mexico. I've never been to New Mexico. I hear the sunsets there are incredible, though. All those pinks and oranges against the desert. 

But no, to answer your question... no unwanted thoughts telling me bad things will happen. I just pace and check the door because... I don't know. I just do.

better.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n mortgage company and my coworkers were monitoring me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve missed social plans, let the townhouse get messier than I can stand, and made enough mistakes at work that my supervisor started checking in on my performance. I’m still working, but it takes much more effort to keep up with basic responsibilities.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 years and 3 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two years and three months, starting after I bought the townhouse and the pressure of keeping it began to build.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 8 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about being watched have been present for about a year and eight months.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 2 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about two years and one month.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 27 / business_operations_specialist / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S030\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad weeks, but not like this... not where it just stays and stays. I guess work can get pretty stressful sometimes, but that's different. I do business operations, so I spend a lot of time staring at spreadsheets and process flows. Spreadsheets can be so frustrating, honestly. If you miss one little comma in a formula, the whole workbook just breaks. I had this one file completely corrupt on me right before a big meeting last year. My manager, David, was practically sweating. He always wears these really loud, patterned ties when he's stressed out. Like, bright orange with tiny blue anchors. I don't even know where you buy ties like that. Probably online. \n\nI buy almost everything online now. I actually just ordered these new curtains for my living room because the streetlights outside my window are so bright, but the color is completely wrong. They looked sage green on the screen, but in person, they're this weird, sickly mint color. I hate returning things through the mail, too. You have to print the label and find a box, and my printer always runs out of black ink right when I need it. It's just such a hassle. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, about feeling depressed before. Um, no. Never for weeks at a time like this. This is definitely new.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking things, like work tasks, locks, or appliances, because you feel responsible if something goes wrong?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's because I feel responsible, but I do check things. A lot, actually. Like... there are times I realize I’ve just been pacing the exact same path, back and forth, from the kitchen to the front door. And I'll be rubbing my thumb against my fingers, just over and over, and I keep checking the peephole. Even though no one knocked and nothing has actually happened. \n\nThe peephole is kind of blurry anyway, to be honest. It's one of those old brass ones that gets all tarnished and scratched up. I tried cleaning it with brass polish once, but the smell gave me this massive headache. It smelled like... I don't know, ammonia and old pennies? I hate the smell of pennies. When I was a kid, my grandpa used to keep this huge glass jar of pennies in his den, and he'd let me roll them in those little paper tubes to take to the bank. The bank tellers always gave me a lollipop for bringing them in. Usually cherry, which is the best flavor, obviously. Grape is the worst. It always tastes like cough syrup. I had to take so much grape cough syrup when I had bronchitis in college. I missed two weeks of classes and had to beg my statistics professor to let me take the midterm late. He was this really stern guy with a mustache that looked completely fake, like it was glued on. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I just pace and check the door. Over and over. It's exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: When you keep checking the door, is it because an unwanted thought keeps telling you something bad will happen if you don’t?\n\nPatient: No, it's not really a thought like that. We just switched to this new software platform for expense reporting, and it is a complete nightmare. The interface is so clunky, and it keeps logging me out every fifteen minutes if I don't move the mouse. I bought one of those mouse jigglers online to keep it active, but it makes this weird whirring noise that drives me crazy. It sounds exactly like the old refrigerator in my first apartment. That thing used to vibrate so loudly I had to put folded up cardboard under the front legs to level it out. Cardboard boxes are so annoying to break down for recycling. You always get those tiny paper cuts right in the webbing of your fingers. \n\nPaper cuts are the absolute worst. I read once that they hurt so much because the edges of paper are actually like microscopic saws, so it tears the skin instead of slicing it cleanly. And there are more nerve endings in your fingertips than almost anywhere else in your body. I learned that in a biology class in college. The professor was this older woman who always wore these massive, chunky turquoise necklaces. She told us she bought them all in New Mexico. I've never been to New Mexico. I hear the sunsets there are incredible, though. All those pinks and oranges against the desert. \n\nBut no, to answer your question... no unwanted thoughts telling me bad things will happen. I just pace and check the door because... I don't know. I just do.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you keep checking the door, does it stop only when it finally feels just right?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Um, no, not really. It doesn't ever feel "just right." It's just... I just eventually get tired and stop. Or I realize how much time has passed. Time is so weird lately, anyway. Like, I'll look at the clock and it's 2:00 PM, and then I swear I just blink and it's suddenly dark outside. 

It gets dark so early now. I hate daylight saving time. I don't understand why we still do it. I read an article that said it was originally for farmers, but then another article said that was a myth and farmers actually hated it because the cows don't care what the clock says. Cows just want to be milked when they want to be milked. My uncle had a dairy farm upstate when I was little. It smelled awful, like manure and sour milk, but he had these barn cats that were so cute. There was this one orange tabby named Marmalade who would just sleep on top of the tractor all day. Tractors are incredibly expensive, by the way. I didn't realize until I had to process an invoice for some agricultural equipment at my last job. The numbers were astronomical. 

But yeah, I don't keep checking until it feels right. I just do it until I stop.

But the house... God, the HOA actually sent me a letter last week about the color of my mailbox. They said it's non-compliant because it's slightly off-white instead of eggshell. Who even notices that? My neighbor, Susan, she has this bright blue door and nobody says a word to her. She's always leaving her trash cans out on the curb for days, too, which I think she does just to annoy the garbage guys. They come really early on Tuesdays, like right when the sun comes up, and the truck is so loud... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? I just... I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I'm ruining everything and I don't deserve my job or my house.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind jumping from one thought to another so much that it’s hard to stay on one topic?\n\nPatient: I don't really think my mind jumps around. I wouldn't say that. It's more like I just get stuck. Like, I get stuck on how bad I am at my job. \n\nYesterday I was trying to run an operations report for the Q3 logistics, and I just kept staring at the spreadsheet. My manager, David, he formats everything with this awful bright green highlight. I don't know why he likes green so much. His car is green, too. It's one of those little hatchbacks, maybe a Subaru? My uncle had a Subaru when I was a kid and the AC was always broken, so whenever we drove up to the lake, we'd just be sweating the whole time. The lake was nice though, very quiet... we used to catch frogs there in the summer. \n\nBut um, no, I don't think my thoughts are jumping or racing. If anything, they just feel really heavy and slow most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or euphoric for days at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt like that. Energetic? Euphoric? God, no. I'm just... tired. All the time. I honestly don't remember the last time I felt actually energetic. \n\nMaybe back in college? I drank a lot of coffee then. There was this little shop right off campus, The Daily Grind, and they had this amazing hazelnut roast. I used to go there with my roommate, Sarah. Sarah was always so energetic, she was a theater major and constantly rehearsing for something. She had this really loud laugh that used to startle me when I was trying to study. We lived in this tiny dorm room on the third floor, and the elevator was always broken, so we had to take the stairs. Carrying groceries up three flights of stairs is the worst, especially when the bags rip. I always double bag now, just in case. \n\nBut yeah, um... no. No periods of feeling energetic or anything like that. Just heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I worry about a lot of different things. It's really just the one thing... how much I'm messing everything up. I mean, I don't sit around worrying about the economy or the weather or anything like that. \n\nWell, actually, I did check the weather app this morning because it looked really cloudy, and I was supposed to get a package delivered today. It's just a new set of mixing bowls. I broke my favorite one last week. It slipped right out of my hands while I was trying to wash it. It was this really pretty ceramic bowl I found at a thrift store in college. I love thrift stores, you never know what you're going to find. Once I found this vintage denim jacket that fit perfectly, but it had this weird smell that I just could not wash out, no matter how much baking soda I used. My mom always swore by baking soda for everything. She used to put an open box in the back of the fridge, which I never really understood, but I guess it works? \n\nAnyway, um... what was the question? Worrying? No, I don't really worry about random things. I just feel like I'm doing everything wrong.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Most of the day, every day, really. It's just this... heaviness. Like I can't even get out of bed sometimes. My bed is actually really uncomfortable, I've been meaning to get a new mattress for like, two years now. I read somewhere that you're supposed to flip them every six months, but mine is one of those pillow-top ones so you can't really flip it, you just rotate it. But it's so heavy, I can't do it by myself. \n\nMy ex-boyfriend used to help me rotate it. He was really into fitness, always at the gym. He drank those protein shakes that smelled like fake chocolate and chalk. We used to go to this smoothie place after his workouts, and I'd always get the mango one. Mangoes are so hard to cut, though, aren't they? There's that giant pit in the middle and you end up wasting half the fruit trying to slice around it. I watched a tutorial on YouTube once about how to cut them with a glass, but I just ended up making a huge sticky mess on my kitchen counter. \n\nBut yeah... down. Just really, really down all the time.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad weeks, but not like this... not where it just stays and stays. I guess work can get pretty stressful sometimes, but that's different. I do business operations, so I spend a lot of time staring at spreadsheets and process flows. Spreadsheets can be so frustrating, honestly. If you miss one little comma in a formula, the whole workbook just breaks. I had this one file completely corrupt on me right before a big meeting last year. My manager, David, was practically sweating. He always wears these really loud, patterned ties when he's stressed out. Like, bright orange with tiny blue anchors. I don't even know where you buy ties like that. Probably online. \n\nI buy almost everything online now. I actually just ordered these new curtains for my living room because the streetlights outside my window are so bright, but the color is completely wrong. They looked sage green on the screen, but in person, they're this weird, sickly mint color. I hate returning things through the mail, too. You have to print the label and find a box, and my printer always runs out of black ink right when I need it. It's just such a hassle. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, about feeling depressed before. Um, no. Never for weeks at a time like this. This is definitely new.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking things, like work tasks, locks, or appliances, because you feel responsible if something goes wrong?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's because I feel responsible, but I do check things. A lot, actually. Like... there are times I realize I’ve just been pacing the exact same path, back and forth, from the kitchen to the front door. And I'll be rubbing my thumb against my fingers, just over and over, and I keep checking the peephole. Even though no one knocked and nothing has actually happened. \n\nThe peephole is kind of blurry anyway, to be honest. It's one of those old brass ones that gets all tarnished and scratched up. I tried cleaning it with brass polish once, but the smell gave me this massive headache. It smelled like... I don't know, ammonia and old pennies? I hate the smell of pennies. When I was a kid, my grandpa used to keep this huge glass jar of pennies in his den, and he'd let me roll them in those little paper tubes to take to the bank. The bank tellers always gave me a lollipop for bringing them in. Usually cherry, which is the best flavor, obviously. Grape is the worst. It always tastes like cough syrup. I had to take so much grape cough syrup when I had bronchitis in college. I missed two weeks of classes and had to beg my statistics professor to let me take the midterm late. He was this really stern guy with a mustache that looked completely fake, like it was glued on. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I just pace and check the door. Over and over. It's exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: When you keep checking the door, is it because an unwanted thought keeps telling you something bad will happen if you don’t?\n\nPatient: No, it's not really a thought like that. It's not like a voice or a specific worry telling me, \"Check the door or else.\" It's more just... this feeling. Like a physical urge to just keep doing it, even though my brain isn't really saying anything. \n\nMy brain usually doesn't shut up, though. It's always going. Mostly about work, honestly. We just switched to this new software platform for expense reporting, and it is a complete nightmare. The interface is so clunky, and it keeps logging me out every fifteen minutes if I don't move the mouse. I bought one of those mouse jigglers online to keep it active, but it makes this weird whirring noise that drives me crazy. It sounds exactly like the old refrigerator in my first apartment. That thing used to vibrate so loudly I had to put folded up cardboard under the front legs to level it out. Cardboard boxes are so annoying to break down for recycling. You always get those tiny paper cuts right in the webbing of your fingers. \n\nPaper cuts are the absolute worst. I read once that they hurt so much because the edges of paper are actually like microscopic saws, so it tears the skin instead of slicing it cleanly. And there are more nerve endings in your fingertips than almost anywhere else in your body. I learned that in a biology class in college. The professor was this older woman who always wore these massive, chunky turquoise necklaces. She told us she bought them all in New Mexico. I've never been to New Mexico. I hear the sunsets there are incredible, though. All those pinks and oranges against the desert. \n\nBut no, to answer your question... no unwanted thoughts telling me bad things will happen. I just pace and check the door because... I don't know. I just do.\n\nDoctor: When you keep checking the door, does it stop only when it finally feels just right?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It doesn't ever feel \"just right.\" It's just... I just eventually get tired and stop. Or I realize how much time has passed. Time is so weird lately, anyway. Like, I'll look at the clock and it's 2:00 PM, and then I swear I just blink and it's suddenly dark outside. \n\nIt gets dark so early now. I hate daylight saving time. I don't understand why we still do it. I read an article that said it was originally for farmers, but then another article said that was a myth and farmers actually hated it because the cows don't care what the clock says. Cows just want to be milked when they want to be milked. My uncle had a dairy farm upstate when I was little. It smelled awful, like manure and sour milk, but he had these barn cats that were so cute. There was this one orange tabby named Marmalade who would just sleep on top of the tractor all day. Tractors are incredibly expensive, by the way. I didn't realize until I had to process an invoice for some agricultural equipment at my last job. The numbers were astronomical. \n\nBut yeah, I don't keep checking until it feels right. I just do it until I stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I really have. I used to do a lot, actually. I used to plan all these weekend brunches, and I'd paint in the spare room, go hiking, or even sign up for networking events for work. But now... I don't know, all those things just feel like chores. They just feel like tasks I’m pretending to care about. I just don't have it in me anymore. 

Especially the networking events. I used to go to so many of them, but they always give you those cheap sticky name tags, and one of them completely ruined my favorite silk blouse. The adhesive just never comes out. Silk is so frustrating to wash anyway. You have to hand wash it in the sink with this special delicate detergent, and the one I bought smells exactly like lavender. It's supposed to be relaxing, I guess, but it just reminds me of my grandmother's linen closet. She kept these little dried lavender sachets tucked between all her guest towels. 

Her towels were always so incredibly scratchy, though. She insisted on line-drying everything in the backyard, even in November. I always wondered how clothes actually dry when it's freezing outside. Wouldn't the water in the fabric just turn to ice? I watched this documentary about glaciers melting in Greenland once, and they talked a lot about ice sublimation. It was actually really sad, but the cinematography was incredible. All these massive chunks of deep blue ice falling into the ocean. The ocean kind of terrifies me, honestly. You just never know what's swimming underneath you in the dark.

Just really, really down all the time.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad weeks, but not like this... not where it just stays and stays. I guess work can get pretty stressful sometimes, but that's different. If you miss one little comma in a formula, the whole workbook just breaks. I had this one file completely corrupt on me right before a big meeting last year. Um, no, not really. I mean, before all this, I was usually pretty good at focusing. I had to be, honestly, with my job. I manage all the operations schedules, so I basically live inside these massive Excel spreadsheets. I used to color-code everything so I could just glance at it and know exactly what was going on. Like, urgent tasks were always this really specific shade of neon pink. 

I love neon pink. It reminds me of this 80s-themed birthday party I went to for my friend Sarah a few years ago. We all went to this retro roller rink out in the suburbs. Roller skating is actually incredibly hard if you haven't done it since you were a kid. I fell right on my tailbone and it bruised so badly. And they served us that really cheap rink pizza, you know the kind? It's basically just cardboard with melted plastic cheese on it, and it is always a million degrees. I burned the entire roof of my mouth on the very first bite. 

It's amazing how fast the inside of your mouth heals, though. I read somewhere that saliva has these special proteins or enzymes that make oral wounds heal way faster than cuts on your skin. Skin is so weird when you actually stop and think about it. It's just this giant organ holding all our insides together. I bought this new body lotion recently that's supposed to be really good for your skin barrier, but the pump on the bottle is totally broken. I have to unscrew the top and bang it against my palm every time I want to use it, which is so annoying when your hands are already slippery. 

But yeah, to answer your question... no, I didn't really have any issues paying attention before. I was always pretty on top of things.

I just pace and check the door because... I don't know. I just do.\n\nDoctor: When you keep checking the door, does it stop only when it finally feels just right?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Like, I'll look at the clock and it's 2:00 PM, and then I swear I just blink and it's suddenly dark outside. \n\nIt gets dark so early now. I hate daylight saving time. I don't understand why we still do it. Cows just want to be milked when they want to be milked. My uncle had a dairy farm upstate when I was little. It smelled awful, like manure and sour milk, but he had these barn cats that were so cute. There was this one orange tabby named Marmalade who would just sleep on top of the tractor all day. Tractors are incredibly expensive, by the way. I didn't realize until I had to process an invoice for some agricultural equipment at my last job. The numbers were astronomical. \n\nBut yeah, I don't keep checking until it feels right. I just do it until I stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really have. I used to do a lot, actually. I used to plan all these weekend brunches, and I'd paint in the spare room, go hiking, or even sign up for networking events for work. But now... I don't know, all those things just feel like chores. They just feel like tasks I’m pretending to care about. I just don't have it in me anymore. \n\nEspecially the networking events. I used to go to so many of them, but they always give you those cheap sticky name tags, and one of them completely ruined my favorite silk blouse. The adhesive just never comes out. It's supposed to be relaxing, I guess, but it just reminds me of my grandmother's linen closet. She kept these little dried lavender sachets tucked between all her guest towels. \n\nHer towels were always so incredibly scratchy, though. She insisted on line-drying everything in the backyard, even in November. I always wondered how clothes actually dry when it's freezing outside. Wouldn't the water in the fabric just turn to ice? I watched this documentary about glaciers melting in Greenland once, and they talked a lot about ice sublimation. It was actually really sad, but the cinematography was incredible. All these massive chunks of deep blue ice falling into the ocean. The ocean kind of terrifies me, honestly. You just never know what's swimming underneath you in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Before you started feeling depressed like this, did you often have trouble paying attention or staying focused?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, before all this, I was usually pretty good at focusing. I had to be, honestly, with my job. I manage all the operations schedules, so I basically live inside these massive Excel spreadsheets. I used to color-code everything so I could just glance at it and know exactly what was going on. Like, urgent tasks were always this really specific shade of neon pink. \n\nI love neon pink. It reminds me of this 80s-themed birthday party I went to for my friend Sarah a few years ago. We all went to this retro roller rink out in the suburbs.
